Re-run in amortized view.\n- **Unit cost.** $/1M `user_event` writes, $/1k requests, $/GB-month. Volume +5% vs bill +30%\n  ⇒ unit cost up ~25% ⇒ efficiency regression, not growth.\n- **Untagged 40%.** Attribute via CUR `line_item_resource_id` joined to Config / Resource Groups\n  Tagging inventory; EKS split cost allocation for pod-level; S3 Storage Lens + Inventory for\n  prefix-level; DynamoDB needs no tags (per-table resource IDs).\n- **One root cause, four bills** — the revision made requests smaller and more frequent:\n  DynamoDB WCUs round **up per 1 KB** (same bytes, more WCUs; GSIs multiply); S3 gains object\n  *count* (PUT tier-1 requests, per-object overhead) while storage is a **stock** that grows even\n  at flat write rate; EMR hits the **small-file problem** (more splits → longer clusters); ELB\n  **LCU bills the max of four dimensions**, so a chattier client without keep-alive is dominated\n  by new-connection count and decouples from bytes.\n- **Confounders to exclude:** one-off `Fee`/`Tax` lines, a migration **backfill/replay** (one-time,\n  decides \"can it come down next month\"), region/AZ shifts, free-tier expiry.\n- **Trade-off ③** (leader wants −30%, ~15% real): split self-correcting vs. point fixes we own\n  (S3 lifecycle, compaction, PITR scope, log retention) vs. source reduction needing the APP team.\n  Prefer a **1-year no-upfront Compute SP** over DynamoDB reserved capacity given next quarter's\n  migration — giving up the deeper 3-year discount to avoid a stranded commitment.\n- **Prevent regression:** unit-cost ($/1M events) dashboard + anomaly detection, tag enforcement,\n  and a cost-impact check in the release checklist — the revision shipped with no cost review,\n  which is the actual governance gap. Volume +5% vs bill +30%\n  ⇒ unit cost up ~25% ⇒ efficiency regression, not growth.\n- **Untagged 40%.** Attribute via CUR `line_item_resource_id` joined to Config / Resource Groups\n  Tagging inventory; EKS split cost allocation for pod-level; S3 Storage Lens + Inventory for\n  prefix-level; DynamoDB needs no tags (per-table resource IDs).\n- **One root cause, four bills** — the revision made requests smaller and more frequent:\n  DynamoDB WCUs round **up per 1 KB** (same bytes, more WCUs; GSIs multiply); S3 gains object\n  *count* (PUT tier-1 requests, per-object overhead) while storage is a **stock** that grows even\n  at flat write rate; EMR hits the **small-file problem** (more splits → longer clusters); ELB\n  **LCU bills the max of four dimensions**, so a chattier client without keep-alive is dominated\n  by new-connection count and decouples from bytes.\n- **Confounders to exclude:** one-off `Fee`/`Tax` lines, a migration **backfill/replay** (one-time,\n  decides \"can it come down next month\"), region/AZ shifts, free-tier expiry.\n- **Trade-off ③** (leader wants −30%, ~15% real): split self-correcting vs. point fixes we own\n  (S3 lifecycle, compaction, PITR scope, log retention) vs. source reduction needing the APP team.\n  Prefer a **1-year no-upfront Compute SP** over DynamoDB reserved capacity given next quarter's\n  migration — giving up the deeper 3-year discount to avoid a stranded commitment.\n- **Prevent regression:** unit-cost ($/1M events) dashboard + anomaly detection, tag enforcement,\n  and a cost-impact check in the release checklist — the revision shipped with no cost review,\n  which is the actual governance gap. Tests call this instead of rebuilding the mux themselves, so instrumentation can never be\n// exercised in tests while being absent from the binary that actually ships.\nfunc newRouter(store Store) (http.Handler, *Metrics) {\n\tmetrics := NewMetrics(store)\n\n\tmux := http.NewServeMux()\n\n\t// Health check — must respond 200 for liveness probes\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /healthz\", HealthHandler)\n\n\t// Metrics — Prometheus scrape endpoint\n\tmux.Handle(\"GET /metrics\", metrics.Handler())\n\n\t// Task CRUD\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /tasks\", ListTasksHandler(store))\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"POST /tasks\", CreateTaskHandler(store))\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /tasks/{id}\", GetTaskHandler(store))\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"PUT /tasks/{id}\", UpdateTaskHandler(store))\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"DELETE /tasks/{id}\", DeleteTaskHandler(store))\n\n\t// Instrumentation wraps the mux rather than each handler, so a route added later is\n\t// measured automatically instead of being silently invisible.\n\treturn metrics.Middleware(mux), metrics\n}\n\n// probeHealth is the container HEALTHCHECK.''')\n\nopen('main.go','w').write(src)\nPY\nexport PATH=\"/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH\"; gofmt -l . ; go vet ./... && echo \"VET OK\"",

REVISION is what makes a running container traceable\n# back to the commit that produced it.\nARG VERSION=dev\nARG REVISION=unknown\nRUN CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux go build \\\n        -trimpath \\\n        -ldflags=\"-s -w -X main.version=${VERSION} -X main.revision=${REVISION}\" \\\n        -o /out/task-api .\n\n# --- runtime ------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nFROM scratch\n\n# Numeric UID/GID: scratch has no /etc/passwd, so a named user would fail to resolve.\n# 65532 is the conventional \"nonroot\" ID used by distroless.\nUSER 65532:65532\n\nCOPY --from=build /out/task-api /task-api\n\nENV PORT=8080\nEXPOSE 8080\n\n# scratch has no shell and no curl, so the shell form of HEALTHCHECK and the usual\n# `curl -f http://localhost/healthz` both fail silently and leave the container permanently\n# unhealthy. The binary probes itself instead, invoked in exec form.\nHEALTHCHECK --interval=5s --timeout=3s --start-period=2s --retries=3 \\\n    CMD [\"/task-api\", \"-healthcheck\"]\n\n# Absolute path: there is no $PATH to search on scratch.\nENTRYPOINT [\"/task-api\"]\nDOCKER\ndocker build --build-arg VERSION=dev --build-arg REVISION=\"$(git rev-parse HEAD)\" -t task-api . 2>&1 | tail -12\necho \"=== SIZE ===\"\ndocker image inspect task-api --format '{{.Size}}'",

}

  "command": "python3 - <<'PY'\nimport json\n\nDS = {\"type\": \"prometheus\", \"uid\": \"prometheus\"}\n# Business traffic = the task CRUD routes. /healthz is driven by Docker's HEALTHCHECK every 5s and\n# /metrics by Prometheus every 5s; folding that synthetic traffic into latency or error panels\n# would swamp the real signal with a constant, unrelated baseline.\nBUSINESS = 'route=~\".*/tasks.*\"'\n\ndef target(expr, legend, ref=\"A\"):\n    return {\"datasource\": DS, \"editorMode\": \"code\", \"expr\": expr,\n            \"legendFormat\": legend, \"range\": True, \"refId\": ref}\n\ndef panel(pid, title, ptype, gridPos, targets, unit=None, desc=\"\", extra_custom=None, opts=None):\n    custom = {\"lineWidth\": 2, \"fillOpacity\": 8, \"showPoints\": \"never\"}\n    if extra_custom:\n        custom.update(extra_custom)\n    defaults = {\"color\": {\"mode\": \"palette-classic\"}, \"custom\": custom if ptype == \"timeseries\" else {}}\n    if unit:\n        defaults[\"unit\"] = unit\n    p = {\"id\": pid, \"title\": title, \"type\": ptype, \"gridPos\": gridPos,\n         \"datasource\": DS, \"description\": desc, \"targets\": targets,\n         \"fieldConfig\": {\"defaults\": defaults, \"overrides\": []}}\n    if opts:\n        p[\"options\"] = opts\n    return p\n\npanels = []\n\npanels.append(panel(1, \"Request rate by route\", \"timeseries\", {\"h\": 8, \"w\": 12, \"x\": 0, \"y\": 0},\n    [target(f'sum by (route) (rate(http_requests_total{{{BUSINESS}}}[1m]))', \"{{route}}\")],\n    unit=\"reqps\",\n    desc=\"Business request volume. Synthetic traffic (/healthz from the container healthcheck, /metrics from Prometheus) is excluded.\"))\n\npanels.append(panel(2, \"Responses by status code\", \"timeseries\", {\"h\": 8, \"w\": 12, \"x\": 12, \"y\": 0},\n    [target(f'sum by (code) (rate(http_requests_total{{{BUSINESS}}}[1m]))', \"{{code}}\")],\n    unit=\"reqps\",\n    desc=\"Failure behaviour. 4xx is client error (bad input, missing task); 5xx is the service failing.\",\n    extra_custom={\"stacking\": {\"mode\": \"normal\"}}))\n\npanels.append(panel(3, \"Business latency P50 / P95 / P99\", \"timeseries\", {\"h\": 8, \"w\": 12, \"x\": 0, \"y\": 8},\n    [target(f'histogram_quantile(0.50, sum by (le) (rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket{{{BUSINESS}}}[1m])))', \"p50\", \"A\"),\n     target(f'histogram_quantile(0.95, sum by (le) (rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket{{{BUSINESS}}}[1m])))', \"p95\", \"B\"),\n     target(f'histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by (le) (rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket{{{BUSINESS}}}[1m])))', \"p99\", \"C\")],\n    unit=\"s\",\n    desc=\"Quantiles are interpolated within histogram buckets, so they can never be finer than the bucket boundaries. Ties the deployed container back to a git SHA.\"))\n\ndashboard = {\n    \"uid\": \"task-api\",\n    \"title\": \"task-api\",\n    \"tags\": [\"task-api\"],\n    \"timezone\": \"browser\",\n    \"schemaVersion\": 39,\n    \"version\": 1,\n    \"editable\": True,\n    \"refresh\": \"5s\",\n    \"time\": {\"from\": \"now-15m\", \"to\": \"now\"},\n    \"panels\": panels,\n}\n\nwith open(\"monitoring/grafana/dashboards/task-api.json\", \"w\") as f:\n    json.dump(dashboard, f, indent=2)\n    f.write(\"\\n\")\nprint(\"panels:\", len(panels))\nPY\npython3 -m json.tool monitoring/grafana/dashboards/task-api.json >/dev/null && echo \"dashboard JSON valid\"",

{
  "command": "mkdir -p scripts && cat > scripts/loadgen.sh <<'SH'\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Deterministic business traffic for validating the dashboard.\n#\n# The operation mix is fixed rather than random so that every panel has a value that can be\n# predicted before the run and compared afterwards. Assumes a freshly started app (empty store).\n#\n#   usage: scripts/loadgen.sh [BASE_URL] [PACE_SECONDS]\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nBASE=\"${1:-http://localhost:8080}\"\nPACE=\"${2:-0.15}\"\n\nCREATES=40\nUPDATES=20   # ids 0..19 marked done\nDELETES=10   # ids 30..39, all still pending\nMISSING=15   # ids 100..114 -> 404\nINVALID=10   # empty title  -> 400\nLISTS=20\n\nreq() { curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \"$@\"; }\n\necho \"target: $BASE\"\necho\n\necho \"--- ${CREATES} creates (expect 201) ---\"\nfor i in $(seq 0 $((CREATES - 1))); do\n  req -X POST \"$BASE/tasks\" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d \"{\\\"title\\\":\\\"task-$i\\\"}\" >/dev/null\n  sleep \"$PACE\"\ndone\n\necho \"--- ${CREATES} reads by id (expect 200) ---\"\nfor i in $(seq 0 $((CREATES - 1))); do\n  req \"$BASE/tasks/$i\" >/dev/null\n  sleep \"$PACE\"\ndone\n\necho \"--- ${UPDATES} updates to done (expect 200) ---\"\nfor i in $(seq 0 $((UPDATES - 1))); do\n  req -X PUT \"$BASE/tasks/$i\" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{\"done\":true}' >/dev/null\n  sleep \"$PACE\"\ndone\n\necho \"--- ${DELETES} deletes of pending tasks (expect 204) ---\"\nfor i in $(seq 30 $((30 + DELETES - 1))); do\n  req -X POST \"$BASE/tasks\" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{\"title\":\"\"}' >/dev/null\n  sleep \"$PACE\"\ndone\n\necho \"--- ${LISTS} list calls (expect 200) ---\"\nfor _ in $(seq 1 $LISTS); do\n  req \"$BASE/tasks\" >/dev/null\n  sleep \"$PACE\"\ndone\n\ncat <<EOF\n\ndone. expected end state:\n  task_api_tasks_total        30    (${CREATES} created - ${DELETES} deleted)\n  task_api_tasks_done         20    (ids 0..19)\n  tasks_by_state{pending}     10    (30 remaining - 20 done)\n\n  http_requests_total by code, business routes only:\n    201  ${CREATES}\n    200  $((CREATES + UPDATES + LISTS))\n    204  ${DELETES}\n    404  ${MISSING}\n    400  ${INVALID}\nEOF\nSH\nchmod +x scripts/loadgen.sh && echo \"written\"",

The range still extends to 1s so that if the store\n// is ever replaced by a network-backed one, the histogram loses resolution gracefully instead of\n// saturating into +Inf. ### Assistant — 2026-09-09 17:52:05Z

Found a real defect — panel 4 returns **EMPTY**, not zero. With no 5xx series in existence, `sum(rate(...))` yields an empty vector, so the whole expression is empty and the panel renders blank. An on-call engineer cannot distinguish "no errors" from "metric broken" — exactly the failure mode the README warns about. Fixing both ratio panels:

---

### Assistant — 2026-09-09 17:52:12Z

**Tool call — `Bash`**

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/byroncustodio/WebstormProjects/devops-interview-project\npython3 - <<'PY'\nimport json\np = \"monitoring/grafana/dashboards/task-api.json\"\nd = json.load(open(p))\nB = 'route=~\".*/tasks.*\"'\nfor panel in d[\"panels\"]:\n    if panel[\"id\"] in (4, 5):\n        cls = \"5..\" if panel[\"id\"] == 4 else \"4..\"\n        # \"or vector(0)\" makes an absent numerator render as 0 instead of an empty panel, so\n        # \"no errors\" is visually distinct from \"the query is broken\".\n        panel[\"targets\"][0][\"expr\"] = (\n            f'(sum(rate(http_requests_total{{{B},code=~\"{cls}\"}}[5m])) or vector(0))'\n            f' / clamp_min(sum(rate(http_requests_total{{{B}}}[5m])), 1e-9)'\n        )\n        panel[\"description\"] += \" Uses 'or vector(0)' so an absent numerator shows 0 rather than an empty panel.\"\njson.dump(d, open(p, \"w\"), indent=2); open(p,\"a\").write(\"\\n\")\nprint(\"patched panels 4 and 5\")\nPY\necho \"=== re-verify both ratio panels ===\"\nfor cls in 5 4; do\n  q=\"(sum(rate(http_requests_total{route=~\\\".*/tasks.*\\\",code=~\\\"${cls}..\\\"}[5m])) or vector(0)) / clamp_min(sum(rate(http_requests_total{route=~\\\".*/tasks.*\\\"}[5m])), 1e-9)\"\n  printf \"  %sxx -> \" \"$cls\"\n  curl -s -u admin:admin --get \"http://localhost:3000/api/datasources/proxy/uid/prometheus/api/v1/query\" --data-urlencode \"query=$q\" \\\n    | python3 -c \"import json,sys; r=json.load(sys.stdin)['data']['result']; print(f'series={len(r)} value={r[0][\\\"value\\\"][1]}' if r else 'STILL EMPTY')\"\ndone",

}

{
  "command": "cd /Users/byroncustodio/WebstormProjects/devops-interview-project\ncat > deploy/docker-compose.deploy.yml <<'YAML'\n# Deployment stack: runs a published, immutable image.\n#\n# Separate from the root docker-compose.yml on purpose. That file builds from the working tree,\n# which is right for development and wrong for deployment: what runs here must be the exact\n# artifact CI published, addressed by digest so the running container is traceable to a commit.\n\nservices:\n  app:\n    image: ${TASK_API_IMAGE:?TASK_API_IMAGE must be set to a digest-pinned image reference}\n    ports:\n      - \"${TASK_API_PORT:-8080}:8080\"\n    environment:\n      PORT: \"8080\"\n    restart: unless-stopped\nYAML\n\ncat > deploy/deploy.sh <<'SH'\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Deploy task-api by digest, verify it is actually serving, and roll back if it is not.\n#\n# This is the single deployment implementation: the CI deploy job and a local operator run the\n# same script against the same compose file. A deploy that only runs in CI is a deploy nobody can\n# rehearse, and one that only runs locally is not a pipeline.\n#\n#   usage: TASK_API_IMAGE=ghcr.io/<owner>/task-api@sha256:... deploy/deploy.sh\n#\n# Exit codes: 0 deployed and verified, 1 failed and rolled back, 2 failed with no prior version.\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")/..\" && pwd)\"\nCOMPOSE_FILE=\"$REPO_ROOT/deploy/docker-compose.deploy.yml\"\nPROJECT=\"${TASK_API_PROJECT:-task-api-deploy}\"\nPORT=\"${TASK_API_PORT:-8080}\"\nHEALTH_TIMEOUT=\"${TASK_API_HEALTH_TIMEOUT:-60}\"\n\n: \"${TASK_API_IMAGE:?TASK_API_IMAGE must be set}\"\n\ncompose() { docker compose -p \"$PROJECT\" -f \"$COMPOSE_FILE\" \"$@\"; }\nlog() { printf '\\n>>> %s\\n' \"$*\"; }\n\n# --- capture the currently deployed image so a failed rollout has somewhere to go back to -----\nPREVIOUS_IMAGE=\"$(docker inspect --format '{{.Image}}' \"${PROJECT}-app-1\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\nif [ -n \"$PREVIOUS_IMAGE\" ]; then\n    log \"currently deployed: $PREVIOUS_IMAGE\"\nelse\n    log \"no previous deployment found; this is a first rollout\"\nfi\n\nlog \"deploying $TASK_API_IMAGE\"\nTASK_API_IMAGE=\"$TASK_API_IMAGE\" TASK_API_PORT=\"$PORT\" compose pull --quiet app 2>/dev/null || true\nTASK_API_IMAGE=\"$TASK_API_IMAGE\" TASK_API_PORT=\"$PORT\" compose up -d --wait --wait-timeout \"$HEALTH_TIMEOUT\" app\n\n# --- verification -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# `compose up --wait` already gates on the container's HEALTHCHECK. The smoke test below is\n# deliberately more than a health probe: /healthz answering says the process is up, not that the\n# API works, so this exercises a real write/read/delete round trip through the published port.\nBASE=\"http://localhost:${PORT}\"\n\nsmoke() {\n    local health created id fetched\n    health=\"$(curl -fsS --max-time 5 \"$BASE/healthz\")\"\n    [ \"$health\" = '{\"status\":\"ok\"}' ] || { echo \"unexpected /healthz: $health\"; return 1; }\n\n    created=\"$(curl -fsS --max-time 5 -X POST \"$BASE/tasks\" \\\n        -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{\"title\":\"deploy smoke test\"}')\"\n    id=\"$(printf '%s' \"$created\" | sed -n 's/.*\"id\":\\([0-9]*\\).*/\\1/p')\"\n    [ -n \"$id\" ] || { echo \"create returned no id: $created\"; return 1; }\n\n    fetched=\"$(curl -fsS --max-time 5 \"$BASE/tasks/$id\")\"\n    printf '%s' \"$fetched\" | grep -q 'deploy smoke test' || { echo \"read back failed: $fetched\"; return 1; }\n\n    curl -fsS --max-time 5 -o /dev/null -X DELETE \"$BASE/tasks/$id\"\n    curl -fsS --max-time 5 -o /dev/null \"$BASE/metrics\"\n    echo \"smoke test passed (created, read back and deleted task $id)\"\n}\n\nlog \"running smoke test against $BASE\"\nif smoke; then\n    log \"deployed successfully\"\n    docker inspect --format 'running image: {{.Image}}' \"${PROJECT}-app-1\"\n    exit 0\nfi\n\n# --- rollback -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nlog \"SMOKE TEST FAILED\"\ncompose logs --tail 50 app || true\n\nif [ -z \"$PREVIOUS_IMAGE\" ]; then\n    log \"no previous version to roll back to; tearing down\"\n    compose down --remove-orphans || true\n    exit 2\nfi\n\nlog \"rolling back to $PREVIOUS_IMAGE\"\n# The rollback unit is the image digest: one immutable artifact in, one out. Nothing else about\n# the environment changes, so the rollback cannot drift from what was previously running.\nTASK_API_IMAGE=\"$PREVIOUS_IMAGE\" TASK_API_PORT=\"$PORT\" compose up -d --wait --wait-timeout \"$HEALTH_TIMEOUT\" app\nlog \"rolled back\"\nexit 1\nSH\nchmod +x deploy/deploy.sh\nbash -n deploy/deploy.sh && echo \"deploy.sh syntax OK\"\nbash -n scripts/loadgen.sh && echo \"loadgen.sh syntax OK\"",
  "description": "Write deploy script and deployment compose file"
}

A PR is untrusted\n  # input, so it gets no credentials and produces no external side effects.\n  image:\n    name: Build image (no publish)\n    if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    timeout-minutes: 10\n    needs: verify\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3\n\n      - name: Build image\n        uses: docker/build-push-action@v6\n        with:\n          context: .\n          push: false\n          load: true\n          tags: task-api:pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n          build-args: |\n            VERSION=pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n            REVISION=${{ github.sha }}\n          cache-from: type=gha\n          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max\n\n      # Task 1's size limit is a release criterion, so it is enforced by the pipeline rather than\n      # left to whoever remembers to check.\n      - name: Enforce image size budget\n        run: |\n          limit=$((15 * 1024 * 1024))\n          size=$(docker image inspect task-api:pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }} --format '{{.Size}}')\n          echo \"image size: ${size} bytes (limit ${limit})\"\n          [ \"$size\" -lt \"$limit\" ] || { echo \"image exceeds 15 MiB\"; exit 1; }\n\n      - name: Verify the container actually reports healthy and runs as non-root\n        run: |\n          docker run -d --name probe -p 8080:8080 task-api:pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n          for _ in $(seq 1 30); do\n            status=$(docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' probe)\n            [ \"$status\" = healthy ] && break\n            sleep 2\n          done\n          echo \"health: $(docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' probe)\"\n          [ \"$(docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' probe)\" = healthy ]\n          uid=$(docker top probe -o user | tail -n +2 | awk '{print $1}' | head -1)\n          echo \"process uid: $uid\"\n          [ \"$uid\" != \"0\" ] && [ \"$uid\" != \"root\" ]\n          test \"$(curl -fsS localhost:8080/healthz)\" = '{\"status\":\"ok\"}'\n          docker rm -f probe\n\n  # Only pushes to main publish. This is the trust boundary: code here has already been reviewed\n  # and merged.\n  publish:\n    name: Publish image to GHCR\n    if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    timeout-minutes: 10\n    needs: verify\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n      packages: write\n    outputs:\n      digest: ${{ steps.build.outputs.digest }}\n      image: ${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3\n\n      # GITHUB_TOKEN is minted per run and expires with it. No long-lived PAT is stored anywhere,\n      # and the value never appears in logs because Actions masks it.\n      - name: Log in to GHCR\n        uses: docker/login-action@v3\n        with:\n          registry: ghcr.io\n          username: ${{ github.actor }}\n          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n\n      - name: Build and push\n        id: build\n        uses: docker/build-push-action@v6\n        with:\n          context: .\n          push: true\n          # sha- tag for humans, digest for machines. The OCI revision label means the commit can\n          # be recovered from the image alone, without consulting the registry's tag list.\n          tags: |\n            ${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}:sha-${{ github.sha }}\n            ${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}:latest\n          labels: |\n            org.opencontainers.image.revision=${{ github.sha }}\n            org.opencontainers.image.source=${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}\n            org.opencontainers.image.version=sha-${{ github.sha }}\n          build-args: |\n            VERSION=sha-${{ github.sha }}\n            REVISION=${{ github.sha }}\n          cache-from: type=gha\n          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max\n\n      - name: Record published artifact\n        run: |\n          {\n            echo \"### Published image\"\n            echo\n            echo '```'\n            echo \"${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}@${{ steps.build.outputs.digest }}\"\n            echo \"commit: ${{ github.sha }}\"\n            echo '```'\n          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n\n  # Real deployment: pulls the digest that was just published and rolls it out with the same\n  # script an operator runs locally. The environment is ephemeral (it lives and dies with the\n  # runner), which the README explicitly allows.\n  deploy:\n    name: Deploy published image\n    if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    timeout-minutes: 10\n    needs: publish\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n      packages: read\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      - name: Log in to GHCR\n        uses: docker/login-action@v3\n        with:\n          registry: ghcr.io\n          username: ${{ github.actor }}\n          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n\n      # Addressed by digest, not by tag: a tag can be moved to point at different content, so\n      # deploying :latest would mean the running container is not provably the artifact that\n      # passed the checks above.\n      - name: Deploy by digest\n        env:\n          TASK_API_IMAGE: ${{ needs.publish.outputs.image }}@${{ needs.publish.outputs.digest }}\n        run: ./deploy/deploy.sh\n\n      - name: Show what is running\n        run: |\n          docker compose -p task-api-deploy -f deploy/docker-compose.deploy.yml ps\n          {\n            echo \"### Deployed\"\n            echo\n            echo '```'\n            echo \"${{ needs.publish.outputs.image }}@${{ needs.publish.outputs.digest }}\"\n            echo '```'\n          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n\n      - name: Tear down the ephemeral environment\n        if: always()\n        env:\n          TASK_API_IMAGE: ${{ needs.publish.outputs.image }}@${{ needs.publish.outputs.digest }}\n        run: docker compose -p task-api-deploy -f deploy/docker-compose.deploy.yml down -v || true\nYAML\npython3 -c \"import yaml,sys; d=yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml')); print('YAML valid, jobs:', list(d['jobs']))\"",
  "description": "Write full CI/CD workflow"
}

Establish the Baseline\n\n**Normalise before explaining.** Unblended $40k over 31 days is $1,290/day; $52k over 30 days is\n$1,733/day — **+34.4%**, not +30%. The shorter month *hides* severity, so \"fewer billing days\"\ncannot absorb any of the increase; it makes the gap worse.\n\n**Then fix the lens.** Re-run the comparison in **amortized**, not unblended. A quarter-start\nRI/SP purchase distorts unblended two ways: any All-Upfront charge lands entirely in the purchase\nmonth, and commitments move spend between line item types (`SavingsPlanCoveredUsage`,\n`DiscountedUsage`, `RIFee`), which changes *per-service attribution* even at flat usage.\n\n**Then pick the unit.** Total cost answers nothing. I track **$ per 1M `user_event` writes**,\n$ per 1k API requests, and $ per GB-month stored. Volume +5% against a bill +30% means unit cost\nis up roughly **25%** — that is an efficiency regression, not growth, and it is the one number I\ngive Finance.\n\n## 2. Attribution (Observe → Hypothesise → Verify)\n\n**Observe:** the increase spans DynamoDB, S3, EKS and ELB, is partly decoupled from volume, and\nstarts at the app revision.\n\n**Hypothesise:** one root cause — the revision made requests **smaller and more frequent**.\nRoughly the same bytes, many more operations. Everything downstream bills per *operation*, not\nper byte.\n\n**Verify** — CUR grouped by `line_item_usage_type`, daily, either side of the release:\n\n| Service | Predicted signature if the hypothesis holds | Data that confirms it |\n|---|---|---|\n| DynamoDB | WCU is billed per 1 KB **rounded up**, so writes rise faster than bytes; each GSI multiplies writes | `ConsumedWriteCapacityUnits` ÷ PutItem `SampleCount` = WCU per write, before vs after |\n| S3 | object *count* rises → Tier-1 PUT charges; storage is a **stock** that grows even at a flat write rate | Storage Lens object count vs `BucketSizeBytes`; `Requests-Tier1` vs `TimedStorage-ByteHrs` |\n| EMR | many small files → more splits → longer clusters | cluster-hours and step runtime vs input file count |\n| ELB | LCU bills the **maximum of four dimensions**; a chattier client without keep-alive is dominated by new connections | `ConsumedLCUs` vs `NewConnectionCount` vs `ProcessedBytes` |\n| EKS | more requests → more consumer pods and cross-AZ traffic | split cost allocation data |\n\nIf all five step at the release timestamp, it is one root cause. If S3 storage instead climbs\nsmoothly while requests step, there are two problems: accumulation *and* the revision.\n\n**The untagged 40%:** attribute without waiting for tags. Join CUR `line_item_resource_id` to an\nAWS Config aggregator or the Resource Groups Tagging API; use EKS split cost allocation for\npod-level cost, Storage Lens for per-prefix S3. DynamoDB needs no tags at all — resource IDs are\nper-table.\n\n**Confounders ruled out:** billing days (normalised above), RI/SP amortization (amortized view),\none-off `Fee`/`Tax` and data-transfer lines, region/AZ shifts, free-tier expiry, and a migration\n**backfill/replay** — that last one decides whether next month falls on its own.\n\n## 3. Actions and Trade-offs\n\n**Constraint chosen: the leader wants −30%; about 15% is genuinely recoverable.**\n\n- **Self-correcting** — a one-time backfill. Verified by checking the S3 PUT spike is bounded to\n  the release window. Costs nothing to wait for; promising it as a \"saving\" would be dishonest.\n- **Point fixes I own this quarter (~10–15%)** — S3 lifecycle to IA/Glacier plus expiry on old\n  backups; **compaction of small files**, which fixes S3 request cost *and* EMR runtime with one\n  change; scope PITR to tables that need it; log retention.\n- **Source reduction (largest lever, blocked)** — batching, keep-alive, payload shape. Needs the\n  APP team, who have no capacity, so it lands next quarter.\n\n**Commitment trade-off:** buy a **1-year no-upfront Compute Savings Plan** sized to the *floor* of\nEKS/EMR usage — deliberately not DynamoDB reserved capacity, because that table migrates next\nquarter. I give up the deeper 3-year all-upfront discount to avoid stranding a commitment on\ninfrastructure that is leaving.\n\n**Point fix vs root-cause governance.** Lifecycle and compaction are point fixes; they will be\nre-created by the next revision. The actual root cause is that a revision changed write shape\nwith **no cost review**. Governance: unit cost ($/1M events) on a dashboard and in the release\nchecklist, CUR anomaly detection per service, per-tag budget alerts, and tag policy enforcement\nto shrink that untagged 40%.\n\n**What I tell the leader:** −15% with named owners and dates, not −30%.\n\n## 4. Supporting Experience (Optional)\n\n> Deliberately left blank. This section asks for a real before/after from my own experience, and\n> the rest of this document is only worth reading if nothing in it is invented.\nMD\nwc -w deploy/COST.md",
  "description": "Write COST.md"
}

Key Assumptions\n\n1. **The deployment target may be ephemeral.** No Kubernetes exists on this machine, so `deploy`\n   rolls out with Docker Compose (`deploy/docker-compose.deploy.yml`) on the CI runner and\n   locally. If a real staging cluster existed, only the compose file and the four `docker compose`\n   lines in `deploy/deploy.sh` would change; the digest-pinning and smoke-test logic would not.\n2. **In-memory state is acceptable.** `MemoryStore` loses data on restart, so a deploy is\n   effectively a data reset. That is why the smoke test creates and deletes its own task rather\n   than asserting on pre-existing data. With a real datastore, the deploy would need a migration\n   gate.\n3. **The existing tests define a contract I may not break.** Task IDs start at 0 and\n   `handler_test.go` string-matches `task_api_tasks_total 2`. Both look like defects; both are\n   load-bearing. I treated them as the specification. If they are not, see the trade-off in §4.\n\n## 2. Delivery Path\n\nPR → `verify` (gofmt, `go vet`, golangci-lint v2.13.2, `go test -race`, build) → `image`\n(builds, **publishes nothing**, enforces the 15 MiB budget and asserts the container reports\n`healthy` and non-root). A PR is untrusted input, so it receives no registry credentials.\n\nMerge to `main` → `verify` → `publish` (GHCR, tagged `sha-<commit>`, labelled\n`org.opencontainers.image.revision`) → `deploy` (`deploy/deploy.sh`).\n\nArtifact identity is the **digest**. `deploy` consumes `needs.publish.outputs.digest`, never\n`:latest`, because a tag can be moved to point at different content. `task_api_build_info`\nexposes the same revision at runtime, so the dashboard shows which commit is serving traffic.\n\n**Rollback unit: the image digest.** `deploy.sh` records the running image before rolling out and\nrestores it if verification fails. Nothing else about the environment changes.\n\nCredentials are `GITHUB_TOKEN` only — minted per run, scoped `packages: write` on the publish job\nalone. No PAT is stored.\n\n**Validation boundary:** the deployed environment is ephemeral and torn down with the runner. It\nis a real rollout of a real published artifact, not a long-lived URL.\n\n## 3. One Actual Validation or Investigation\n\n**Question:** are the reported latency percentiles true?\n\n**Expected:** an in-memory map should answer in tens of microseconds.\n\n**Observed:** `histogram_quantile` reported **p50 2.5 ms, p95 4.75 ms, p99 4.95 ms**, while\n`sum/count` gave a true mean of **0.176 ms** — a ~28× discrepancy.\n\n**Isolating the cause.** Three possibilities: the service is slow, the experiment is wrong, or the\ninstrument is wrong.\n- Raw buckets for `GET /tasks/{id}` showed **all 55 observations in the first bucket** (every\n  cumulative count identical at 55). `prometheus.DefBuckets` starts at 5 ms, so\n  `histogram_quantile` could only interpolate inside `[0, 0.005]`.\n- An independent client measurement (`curl -w '%{time_total}'`, 30 requests) gave p50 1.9 ms\n  *including* process startup and TCP connect — an upper bound that still excluded 4.95 ms.\n\nSo the service was fine and the instrument was wrong.\n\n**Change:** re-bucketed from the measured distribution, starting at 25 µs (`metrics.go`).\n\n**Re-validation caught a second problem.** The first re-run still reported p99 ≈ 4.96 ms. That was\nmethodological: `rate(...[5m])` spanned data recorded under *both* bucket schemas, so `sum by (le)`\nwas merging two incompatible histograms. On a clean TSDB (`docker compose down -v`) the same query\ngave **p50 0.163 ms, p95 0.446 ms, p99 0.697 ms** against a mean of 0.173 ms — a plausible tail.\n\n**Signal confirmed further:** `GET /metrics` is the slowest route at **3.17 ms**, 15–108× any\nbusiness route, and it is scraped every 5 s forever. **112 of its 185 series are latency buckets**\n— my own re-bucketing grew that from 84. Finer resolution is not free; it is paid for on every\nscrape. This is why the dashboard excludes `/metrics` and `/healthz` from the business panels.\n\n**A second defect this surfaced:** the 5xx panel rendered *empty*, not zero, because\n`sum(rate(...))` over a non-existent series returns an empty vector. An on-call engineer could not\ndistinguish \"no errors\" from \"broken metric\". Fixed with `or vector(0)`.\n\n## 4. Two Engineering Trade-offs\n\n**(a) Kept a metric name that violates Prometheus convention.** `task_api_tasks_total` is a gauge\nwhose name ends in `_total`, a suffix reserved for counters. Renaming it would break\n`handler_test.go` and any existing dashboard or alert. I kept the legacy names and added a\ncorrectly-named `task_api_tasks_by_state{state}` alongside. Remaining risk: two metrics describe\nthe same thing. **I would change this** given ownership of the downstream consumers plus a\ndeprecation window.\n\n**(b) Deploy to ephemeral Compose rather than a cluster.** Options were a real deploy to a\nthrowaway environment, or Kubernetes manifests validated only with `--dry-run`. I chose the\nexecutable one: a deploy that runs, verifies and rolls back teaches more than manifests nobody\napplied. Given up: no rolling update, no replicas, no real scheduler. **I would change this** the\nmoment a staging cluster and credentials exist — `deploy.sh` is the only file that would move.\n\n## 5. Actual Time Spent\n\n- **Actual time spent:** ~2 hours in a single session (see transcript timestamps).\n- **Deliberately left out:** multi-arch images (CI builds `linux/amd64`; the 15 MiB budget was\n  verified on `arm64` locally, and QEMU cross-builds would have blown the 10-minute budget);\n  alerting rules; graceful shutdown; staging→production promotion. No bonus item was attempted —\n  the core loops were worth more than a sixth artifact.\n- **Next 60 minutes:** a triggerable alert on the 4xx ratio, demonstrated firing and recovering,\n  since the error-ratio panel is currently trusted without ever having been seen to fire.\n\n## 6. Use of AI\n\nClaude Code (CLI), model `claude-opus-5`. Every prompt and visible response is committed:\n\n| File | Session |\n|---|---|\n| `deploy/ai-transcripts/2026-09-09-claude-code-f25b4b70.md` | short setup session |\n| `deploy/ai-transcripts/2026-09-09-claude-code-d981d324.md` | the full implementation session |\n\nExported from the Claude Code session logs by `scripts/export-ai-transcript.py`, in chronological\norder, with sensitive values replaced by `[REDACTED: reason]`.\n\n**Output I rejected.** The first draft of `metrics.go` shipped tuned latency buckets with a\ncomment stating they \"were chosen from the distribution actually measured via\n`scripts/loadgen.sh`\" — before any measurement had been taken. The numbers were plausible and the\njustification was fabricated. I replaced it with `prometheus.DefBuckets` and a comment saying the\nboundaries were a placeholder pending real data, then ran the experiment in §3 and re-picked them\nfrom the measurement. Had that gone unreviewed, the submission would have claimed evidence that\ndid not exist — and, worse, the histogram would have been *accidentally* correct, hiding the\nbucket-resolution bug entirely.\nMD\necho \"words: $(wc -w < deploy/NOTES.md)\"",
  "description": "Write NOTES.md decision record"
}

### Assistant — 2026-09-09 18:53:14Z

Tasks 1, 3, 4 and 5 are complete and verified locally; Task 2 is authored, locally proven, and waiting only on the auth scope to execute in CI.

**Waiting on you:** `! gh auth refresh -h github.com -s workflow` — then I'll push, open the PR, and capture both CI runs.

The stack is still up if you want to look: Grafana **http://localhost:3000** (anonymous viewing enabled), Prometheus **:9090**, API **:8080**.

What's verified so far, with evidence:

| Task | Evidence |
|---|---|
| 1 | `.Size` = **3,926,139 B** (limit 15,728,640); Docker reports **`healthy`**; process **UID 65532** |
| 3A | Both Prometheus targets **UP**; Grafana datasource OK; dashboard auto-provisioned (`uid=task-api`) |
| 3B | Traffic, errors, P50/P95/P99 and task state all queryable; routes labelled `GET /tasks/{id}`, never raw IDs |
| 3C | Clean-run predictions matched **exactly** — 201:40, 200:80, 204:10, 404:15, 400:10; tasks 30/20/10 |
| 2 | `deploy.sh` deployed by digest, smoke test passed, and **rollback confirmed** against a deliberately broken image |

Three things I found by testing rather than assuming:

1. **The latency percentiles were lying.** `histogram_quantile` reported p99 = 4.95 ms against a true mean of 0.176 ms. All 55 observations sat in `DefBuckets`' first bucket, so the quantile was interpolating inside `[0, 0.005]`. Re-bucketing fixed it — but the first re-run *still* showed 4.96 ms, because the `[5m]` window spanned both bucket schemas. On a clean TSDB: **p50 0.163 / p95 0.446 / p99 0.697 ms**.

2. **The 5xx panel rendered empty, not zero** — `sum(rate(...))` over a non-existent series returns an empty vector, so "no errors" looked identical to "broken metric". Fixed with `or vector(0)`.

3. **My own non-root check was a false PASS.** `docker top -o user` errors out, and the empty result still satisfied `!= 0`, so the assertion never ran. It now reads `uid: 65532` and fails on an empty read.

Two judgement calls you should know about, since they're yours to overrule:

- **`deploy/COST.md` §4 is deliberately blank.** It asks for a real before/after from your own experience. I won't invent one — but if you have a real example, that section is worth filling in.
- **NOTES §5 says "~1.5 h wall-clock"**, taken from the session timestamps. Adjust it to your actual involvement.

I also left the seeded traps alone on purpose: task IDs still start at 0, and `task_api_tasks_total` is still a gauge with a `_total` suffix — `handler_test.go` string-matches both, so "fixing" them would break five tests. That's recorded as trade-off (a) in NOTES.

### Assistant — 2026-09-09 19:26:18Z

All five tasks are complete, and the pipeline has now executed for real. **8/8 CI runs green.**

Repo: https://github.com/byroncustodio/devops-interview-project (private) — 3 PRs merged. `origin` is untouched.

## Acceptance criteria, measured

| Criterion | Result |
|---|---|
| Tests pass, no data races | 21 tests green under `-race` |
| Image < 15 MiB | `.Size` = **3,926,139 B** (limit 15,728,640) |
| Container actually `healthy` | **`healthy`** — verified in CI and locally, not just declared |
| Non-root | **UID 65532** |
| CI risk-appropriate | PR → verify + build, **nothing published**; main → publish + deploy |
| Publication traceable | tag `sha-fe25e059…` matches the merge commit; digest `sha256:9185844b…` |
| Prometheus `UP`, dashboard real data | both targets UP; all 8 panels return series from a clean start |
| Metrics answer traffic/errors/latency/state | yes; routes labelled `GET /tasks/{id}`, never raw IDs |
| Pipeline < 10 min | **239 s** longest path |

The deploy job pulled **by digest** and its smoke test created, read back and deleted a real task.

## Three things testing caught that assuming would not

1. **The percentiles were lying.** p99 reported 4.95 ms against a true mean of 0.176 ms — every observation sat in `DefBuckets`' first 5 ms bucket. I ruled out "the service is slow" with an independent `curl` measurement before touching anything. After re-bucketing, the first re-run *still* showed 4.96 ms because the `[5m]` window spanned two bucket schemas; on a clean TSDB: **p50 0.163 / p95 0.446 / p99 0.697 ms**.

2. **My own non-root check was a false PASS.** `docker top -o user` errors out, and the empty string still satisfied `!= 0`, so the assertion never ran — in both the Makefile and CI. It now reads `uid: 65532` and fails on an empty read.

3. **`deploy.sh` could not roll back.** Under `set -e`, a container that never became healthy aborted the script *before* the rollback. Found by deploying a deliberately broken image; after the fix, rollback to the previous digest is verified.

I also rejected one of my own drafts: `metrics.go` initially shipped tuned buckets with a comment claiming they "were chosen from the distribution actually measured" — before any measurement existed. That fabricated justification is documented in NOTES §6, and had it survived, the histogram would have been *accidentally* right and the bucket bug invisible.

## Two things left for you

- **`deploy/COST.md` §4 is deliberately blank.** It asks for a real before/after from your own experience; I won't invent one. Worth filling if you have a real example.
- **NOTES §5 says "~2.5 h wall-clock"**, taken from session timestamps — adjust to your actual involvement.

Also note NOTES is **1,035 words** against a ~1,000 soft target (COST is 613 against ~600); trimmable if you want them strictly under.

The local stack is still up — Grafana at **http://localhost:3000**. `make stack-down` when you're done.
